# Integration Tests
> Cross-module and cross-SDK compatibility testing

## Overview

This directory contains integration tests that verify the BSV Rust SDK works correctly across modules and produces results identical to the TypeScript and Go SDK implementations. Tests use shared JSON test vectors to ensure byte-for-byte compatibility across all three SDK implementations.

**Total: 1,332 integration tests across 47 test files (`cargo test --features "full,http,websocket"` at `1eff2d0`, 2026-10-05: 3,014 passed with the 1,513 unit and 169 doc tests; the 5 `memory_profiling` tests run behind `dhat-profiling`, alone, outside the count) + 2,049 JSON test vectors across the twelve files below (README's 2,031 counts the JSON arrays' lengths: `overlay_types.json` is an object of six categories) + 54 wallet-wire message files + Rust constant vectors**

`.github/scripts/test_front_pages.py` holds no Rust binary: the unit tests of `.github/scripts/front_pages.py`, the GitHub Release page generator (`notes`, `flags`) and the front pages' reconciler (`check`), run by the `front-pages` gate of `checks`, by `release.yml`'s `page` job and by `front-pages.yml`, on the saved answers of `.github/scripts/fixtures/front_pages/` (a synthetic world in which every rule passes; each test plants one change and reads the rule RED, a planted draft advisory among them, which no page may carry; a tree for each kind of tag: a stable patch fixing an advisory, one fixing none, a prerelease, a back-patch, an old release inside three ranges, a release with no run id; `Producers` reads, from this tree, the producer of every pattern the measured block looks for, so a renamed job or summary line fails on its pull request; 88 tests: since #886 a rule whose source did not answer read once more from the state before its failed attempt and red only on a second miss (`Retry`, three tests on a source whose named method raises once or twice; `Answers`, three: a 404 a source reads as an answer read once with no pause, crates.io's 404 for the crate and a `gh` 404 of `repo`, `tags` or `releases` `Unavailable` and read again), and a version crates.io lists as yanked left out of the newest stable by the one function `newest_stable`, which `flags` and `check`'s `versions` rule both ask (`NewestStable`, two, one reading the script's source for its two callers; a yanked own version never taking the mark; a yanked version's tag not the newest stable tag, the last two RED on `1592e699`'s script); since #883's second fold docs.rs's own answers of 2026-11-11 under `docsrs_answers/` (a queued release's 404, a build in progress, a failed build, a built release), a mixed failure after the wait red, crates.io ahead of every tag permanent, a manifest that did not ask docs.rs for every feature `not judged`, the five untagged versions pinned, an unreadable run `not recorded`); `expected/notes-v0.8.7.md` is the whole page of its stable patch, regenerated by `python3 -B .github/scripts/front_pages.py --offline .github/scripts/fixtures/front_pages notes v0.8.7 --run 7` and read before it is committed.

`tests/sibling/` holds no test binary: `column.json`, this crate's recorded column on the sibling's (`bsv-script-lean`) nine corpora, read by `.github/scripts/sibling_harness.py` in `ci.yml`'s `sibling` job (#97), and `PIN`, the sibling commit the job checks out (one line of 40 hex digits, since #97's judge lane; the job's `pin` step reads it); its manual is `tests/sibling/CLAUDE.md`. `.github/scripts/test_sibling_judge.py` and `test_sibling_harness.py` hold no Rust binary either: the unit tests of `sibling_judge.py` (the node oracle's judge, run by `sibling-judge.yml` on the artifact `sibling-run.yml` uploads: `judge`, `report`, `sweep`, the dry run; 104 hermetic tests since fix-97c (one verdict per head: a judgement judges the newest stage R run of its head and waits for one still running, `Interleavings`, one test per row of the table in `docs/lanes/fix-97c.md`; `report` not trusting the judge's `post=no` where its own reading owes a check; the sweep's newest stage R run by path, event and head repository, its `judge in progress` and the act for an unparsed stage R), 92 since the wiring lane's folds (each stamp and record comparison alone, the dispatch's stamp, a line split on a line feed alone, the branch quoted, a check standing only under the App's id, a re-run judged from its own attempt's artifact, at the instant its attempt started and to the second, the start and the artifact's creation parsed and compared as times (an unparsed one not judged), the re-push act on a line of its own, the line named behind by the trampoline's two commit times (read as integers, or not judged), stage R running beta's harness), 74 before, one table per rule, a fake of its one GitHub class and git objects built in a temporary directory, the two run records of 2026-11-12 under `.github/scripts/fixtures/sibling_judge/`) and of `sibling_harness.py` (`run`, `raw`, `compare`, `judge_run`; 73), run by the `judge-tests` gate of `checks` on every pull request.

## Files

**A row's modules (#706).** Every top-level `tests/*.rs` has a row in the table below, and its Modules cell (the fourth) names, in backticks, the crate modules the file reaches, by two clauses. The guarded clause: at least every module its source names by a `bsv_rs::<m>` path or a crate-root re-export named through the crate (`bsv_rs::Transaction`, `bsv_rs::sha256`, an item of a `bsv_rs::{...}` group), over the binary and the module files it declares; `none` when it names none. That clause cannot move a selection, since the nightly's direct reading and a pull request's plan read the same paths from the source already (`used_modules`); it makes the table complete. The caller clause: a binary that reaches a module only through a caller (it names `bsv_rs::transaction`, and the transaction code calls `crate::script`) names that module where its description says it exercises it (a BEEF binary that executes scripts names `script`). That clause is a judgment, and the only part of a row that moves a selection: the writer applied it once by a word list (`CALLER_WORDS` in `.github/scripts/rows_from_sources.py`), and the six pairs it decided are frozen in `tests/caller_modules_706.txt`, which the guard and `--check` both read; a new pair is the author's to add there. A row shared by several files names each file's modules apart (`` `a.rs`: `m`, `n`; `b.rs`: none ``), each from that file's own source and no caller's (the description is every file's): the row's union is no file's reading. A path in the description (`src/<m>/`, `` `<m>::` ``) names no module: the cell is the reading. The nightly's run selection reads the rows this way, beside the binaries' `bsv_rs::` paths (#685); a pull request's plan reads them with one level of callers (`.github/scripts/affected_tests.py`). `tests/arch_tests_claude_rows_name_modules_706.rs` refuses a binary without a row, a row naming fewer than its source, a shared row without a part for each file and a row dropping a frozen pair; `python3 .github/scripts/rows_from_sources.py` writes the cells from the sources, keeps what a cell already names for its file and always writes the frozen pairs (`--check` refuses as the guard does, and the Test Suite's plan step runs it, #708); on a tree `--check` passes the writer is the identity, byte for byte: it rewrites only a Modules cell that moves, and every reader of the table (the scripts' `nightly_mutants.cell_spans`, the guard's `cells`) reads a `|` inside a code span (a run of n backticks to the next run of exactly n) or after a backslash outside one as text, where until the rows lane a `||` inside backticks was two more cells and the writer wrote it back spaced (`.github/scripts/test_rows_from_sources.py`, 10 tests: the reader's cases, a round-trip property over 2,000 generated rows and the writer run on a copy of the tree with an empty diff, run in the plan step beside `--check`). One row per file: a stem named in two rows is refused by both, naming its files and each row's line, since every reader keys the table by the stem (`support/a.rs` and `a.rs` are both `a`) and keeps the last copy, so an edit to the other is never read (#712).

`auth_response_order_400.rs`: three tests against Node 22.18.0 / ts-stack@edf6e03 recorded certificateResponse bytes. Numeric field/keyring names are reserialized in JavaScript order; the typed fallback preserves nonnumeric insertion order without retained text or a sorted Value. All RED on base 8e57ee46 with serde_json's default, then green; the third checks class property order and omission of an extra property. The #280 text and #394 ordinary-lookup pins remain, and all existing fixtures retain beta's bytes. Recorder: `support/auth_response_order_400.mjs`.

the bound, as its header writes it: These machines keep a pinned type's attribute, fields and doc from going stale unnoticed by an ordinary change: one made to the type, its fields, its attribute or its doc for a reason of its own. They are not a defence against a change built to defeat them (a pinned text edited together with its pin, a digest with its constant, a doc attribute written to hide or stand in for a block, a doc suite that is not run): such a change is visible in its diff and is a reviewer's matter. ## Test Vectors

Test vectors in `tests/vectors/` are shared with the TypeScript and Go SDKs:

Under the #280 precedent this crate keeps the received signed bytes: H3 (whitespace header signed raw) verifies here/refuses in both references; H3b (whitespace header signed over its stringified parsed object) refuses here/verifies there; H5 (escaped `\u0074ypes` key signed raw) verifies here/refuses there; H5b (escaped-key header signed over its stringified parsed object) refuses here/verifies there. Four behavior pins read these recorded reference verdicts, including the previously unused whitespace case; no RED is expected. Neither writer emits these whitespace or redundant-escape forms: the reference's `ts-stack@edf6e03` `packages/middleware/auth-express-middleware/src/index.ts:943,1015` uses `JSON.stringify`, this crate's `src/auth/transports/http.rs:582` uses `serde_json::to_string`. Re-serialising on receipt as `packages/sdk/src/auth/Peer.ts:1068` does remains an alternative open for the keeper's word. | `bump_invalid.rs` | Invalid BUMP vectors with expected error messages |
| `bigtx.rs` | Large transaction test vectors |
| `beef_cross_sdk.rs` | BEEF (BRC-62) and MerklePath vectors from TypeScript/Go SDKs |
| `atomic-beef.json` | BRC-95 vectors in the ts-stack corpus format, staged for `vectors/sdk/transactions/` (six: Go's dependency order, TS's partition order, a raw unproven ancestor after its proven descendant (TS), the subject not last (this crate's writer), an unrelated entry (TS's writer, refused), the subject missing (hand-built, refused), and the X/Y pair twice (a raw `X` nothing spends and its parent `Y`, both outside the graph; this crate's test writer, refused; the stream names the earlier by the trie order: `Y` in one, `X` in the other)) |

## Test Categories by Module

### Primitives (default features)
- **`cross_sdk_tests.rs`** — BRC-42 key derivation, symmetric encryption, WIF/address vectors, DRBG verification, edge cases (unicode, empty, large messages)
- **`drbg_tests.rs`** — NIST vector tests, SHA-512 variant, reseed, output length, determinism
- **`ec_tests.rs`** — BRC-42 derivation consistency, WIF/pubkey/address known vectors, sign/verify, ECDH, PublicKey::is_valid(), get_public_key from P2PK scripts, key serialization
- **`integration_tests.rs`** — Full workflows: key derivation, symmetric encryption, Schnorr proofs, Shamir secret sharing (3-of-5, subset recovery, the `_with` form's coefficients evaluated at BRC-140's drawn x coordinates, #854), P-256, hash chains, sighash+signing, complete payment workflow
- **`shamir_backup_854.rs`** — BRC-140 backup shares against the reference's recording (`vectors/shamir_backup_854.json`) in both directions, the reader's refusals in its words, and `recover_private_key_from_legacy_backup` on shares bsv-rs 0.8.5 wrote; the zero secret, the reduction modulo n and BRC-140's coordinates cross-recorded (#854 and its fold)
- **`shamir_interpolation_880.rs`**: the batched interpolation against the pairwise one kept as the oracle: 10,000 seeded share sets at both recovery doors (keys and refusals, one pinned digest), the public `Polynomial::value_at` on hand-built points, and the cost ratio at 128 of 128 (#880)

### Script (default features)
- **`script_vectors_tests.rs`** — 458 spend valid vectors, 598 script parsing vectors, 432 invalid script vectors, individual opcode tests (OP_CAT, OP_SPLIT, OP_MUL, OP_DIV)
- **`sighash_tests.rs`** — 499 sighash vectors (from 500 JSON entries) with `compute_sighash`, detailed first-vector debugging, transaction parsing verification
- **`template_tests.rs`** — P2PKH (lock/unlock/address/validation), P2PK (compressed detection, length estimate), Multisig (2-of-3, 1-of-1, 3-of-3, 16-of-16, validation errors), RPuzzle (hash types, K value, R computation)

### Transaction (`transaction` feature)
- **`transaction_tests.rs`** — Parsing/roundtrip (104 tests), fee models (Fixed, SatoshisPerKilobyte), MockChainTracker, broadcast, MerklePath/BUMP, BEEF format, ancestry collection (`to_beef`, `to_atomic_beef`), SPV verification (`Transaction::verify()`), cross-SDK BEEF/MerklePath vectors. Organized into submodules: `beef_extended_tests`, `beef_ancestry_tests`, `cross_sdk_tests`, `merkle_path_advanced_tests`

- **`beef_stream.rs`**: The streaming BEEF reader against the rows of the Lean definition `BeefOfAnySize` (bsv-stack-lean): each refusal with its offset and kind, the BRC-62 example cut and accepted with its script executed, the resumption at every `k`, chunks of every size, the spend refusals, the asynchronous reader; a raw transaction with no input refused as `NoInputs` at its offset, unproven beside a proven stranger and under a BUMP (0.4.1, bsv-stack-lean #58)
- **`beef_deep_chain.rs`**: the P0-5 chain's walks on a 1 MiB stack; written newest first, valid on the three readers in linear time (#240); the `to_beef` round trip at 25,000 links on a 256 KiB stack, the same links per byte (speed loop two, #670; a recursion per link planted in `to_beef` overflows it)
- **`merkle_path_cost.rs`**: the parse of a BUMP of 8,192 leaves (flat and a compound one from a block of 2^14) under 10 s in a debug build, the tree height over 64 refused, `{:?}` of a linked chain on a 1 MiB stack (bsv-stack-lean #57, P0-5c); the compound BUMP's root asked of nine txids spread over the block, the last included (85, every 97th, until speed loop two, #670, ten of the test's eleven seconds)
- **`beef_stream_deep.rs`**: The P0-5 chain at 1,000, 10,000 and 100,000 links read from a source that writes itself (`support/beef_chain.rs`) on a 1 MiB stack, the resumption at three points, the P0-5c wide BUMP at 8,192 and 16,384 leaves, the Lean's counts at those sizes
- **`beef_limits.rs`**: `BeefLimits` as memory hints: a BEEF over every one is read, a claimed count is refused for the bytes

### Conformance corpus (`transaction` feature; `$BSV_CONFORMANCE_DIR`, skipped loudly when absent)
- **`conformance_scripts.rs`** — The ts-stack corpus's script domain: 5,116 evaluation vectors (node script fixtures from bitcoin-sv and Teranode, the SDK's), the sighash and script regressions; every mismatch classified into an enumerated `unsupported` class or a pinned known failure, the per-class counts pinned (`EVALUATION_UNSUPPORTED_PINS`, `EVALUATION_KNOWN_FAILURES`), so corpus or interpreter drift is a red test. CI's `vectors` job (required on both lines) runs it against the corpus at `ts-stack@edf6e03`, its `TS_STACK_PIN` (Calhooon/bsv-rs#31); the other CI jobs see no corpus and skip
- **`conformance_beef.rs`** — The corpus's BUMP (MerklePath), serialization, BEEF and transaction-regression suites, the same classification and pins

### Transaction HTTP (`transaction`+`http` features, wiremock)
- **`broadcaster_http_tests.rs`** — ARC (10 tests: success, API key, errors, batch), WoC (9 tests: mainnet/testnet/STN, errors), Teranode (8 tests: EF format, timeout, batch)
- **`chaintracker_http_tests.rs`** — WoC tracker: valid/invalid root, case-insensitive, 404/500, current_height, testnet paths
- **`live_policy_http_tests.rs`** — LivePolicy dynamic fee model: refresh from a policy endpoint in ARC's schema, no default URL (#350), error handling (500/404/malformed), fallback rates, cache expiry, sat/byte→sat/KB conversion, API key auth

### Wallet (`wallet` feature)
- **`wallet_tests.rs`** — KeyDeriver (14 tests: derivation, validation, security levels), CachedKeyDeriver (8 tests: LRU eviction, secrets not cached), ProtoWallet (16 tests: encrypt/decrypt, HMAC, signatures, cross-party), cross-SDK BRC-2/BRC-3 compliance (4 tests), wire protocol encoding (14 tests: VarInt, strings, Counterparty, Protocol, Outpoint, maps, enums)
- **`wire_method_roundtrip_tests.rs`**: All 28 WalletInterface methods via loopback transport (106 tests): get_public_key, encrypt/decrypt, HMAC, signatures, key linkage, status (auth/height/network/version), actions (create/sign/abort/list/internalize), outputs (list/relinquish), certificates (acquire/list/prove/relinquish/discover). Also tests complex type roundtrips, response roundtrips, call codes, request frames, and Counterparty variant encoding.
- **`wallet_wire_cross_sdk_tests.rs`**: Cross-SDK wire protocol compatibility with Go SDK (55 tests; 13 need `http`, the recording wallet of `support/fixtures`). Loads 54 JSON vector files from Go SDK testdata. Tests request decoding (params extraction after call_code + originator) and response decoding (result extraction after error byte) for all 28 WalletInterface methods. Validates byte-level interoperability between Rust and Go wire encoders.

### Messages (`messages` feature)
- **`messages_tests.rs`** — BRC-77 signed messages (8 tests: specific/anyone recipient, empty/large, tampering), BRC-78 encrypted messages (9 tests: roundtrip, wrong key, GCM auth), cross-SDK vectors (8 tests: error format compatibility), unicode/binary data, multi-party, the `_with` forms (4 tests: the plain forms' bytes given their drawn key id and IV, fixed values reproducible)

### Compat (`compat` feature)
- **`compat_bip39_tests.rs`** — TREZOR vectors: entropy↔mnemonic (22 vectors), mnemonic→seed, validation (bad sentences, wrong word count, bad checksum), generation (12/15/18/21/24 words), randomized roundtrips (100 iterations per entropy size)
- **`compat_integration_tests.rs`** — BIP-39+BIP-32 HD wallet flows, BSM sign/verify, ECIES (Electrum/Bitcore), Base58, cross-module integration, error handling, type/enum tests

### Auth (`auth` feature)
- **`auth_cross_sdk_tests.rs`** — Certificate binary/JSON roundtrip, deterministic serialization, field sorting, DER signature format, outpoint parsing, cross-SDK binary layout
- **`auth_integration_tests.rs`** — SessionManager lifecycle/pruning, AuthMessage validation/signing, mock transport, HTTP payloads (complex/unicode/100 headers), certificate signing/verification, RequestedCertificateSet, PeerSession states, nonce handling (TS SDK `initial_nonce` fallback), error propagation through oneshot channels
- **`auth_peer_e2e_tests.rs`** — Full BRC-31 mutual authentication between two Peer instances via channel loopback: handshake, bidirectional messaging, session persistence, explicit `get_authenticated_session()`, listener registration, certificate request/response flow, empty/large (100KB) payloads, nonce uniqueness, error cases (invalid version, no session)

### Overlay (`overlay` feature)
- **`overlay_cross_sdk_tests.rs`** — Admin token creation/decoding/protocol detection, network presets, LookupQuestion/Answer types, AdmittanceInstructions, TaggedBEEF
- **`overlay_integration_tests.rs`** — Protocols, LookupQuestion/Answer, TaggedBEEF, AdmittanceInstructions, TopicBroadcaster validation, RequireAck, LookupResolverConfig, HostReputationTracker (ranking, reset, JSON export/import, global singleton), SyncHistorian (chain traversal, filtering, cycle prevention), admin tokens, HostResponse/ServiceMetadata, LookupResolver.find_competent_hosts() public API
- **`overlay_admin_token_ts_parity_tests.rs`** — `create_signed_overlay_admin_token` byte-exact against `@bsv/sdk 1.10.1`'s `pushdrop.lock` output on five recorded cases
- **`overlay_mock_tests.rs`** — MockLookupFacilitator and MockBroadcastFacilitator with pre-configured responses. Tests LookupResolver with mock SLAP facilitators (query routing, empty results, error handling, call counting), TopicBroadcaster with mock SHIP facilitators (broadcast routing, multi-host fan-out, partial failures, RequireAck handling). No network access required.
- **`lookup_slap_admission_786.rs`** — the SLAP discovery admits a host only from a 1-satoshi output whose `txid` hint agrees or is absent, as the reference does (#786; scripted facilitator, `.invalid` trackers, the reference's recorded admissions)
- **`overlay_ship_discovery_804.rs`** — the SHIP discovery's admission (answer and BEEF bounds, the txid hint, 1 satoshi, the endpoint's origin, 32 hosts) and the specific-host keys' snapshot, as the reference does (#804; an `.invalid` host override, the reference's recorded verdicts)
- **`overlay_ship_requirements_tb.rs`**: the acknowledgment requirements as the reference has them: the requirement topics checked at construction, every interested host seeded, the three failure codes (lane fix-tb-acks; scripted resolver and facilitator, the reference's recorded verdicts)

### Overlay HTTP (`overlay`+`http` features, wiremock)
- **`overlay_http_tests.rs`** — SHIP broadcast facilitator (25 tests: STEAK response, Content-Type/X-Topics headers, off-chain values, error responses 400/404/500, HTTP security, URL trimming) and SLAP lookup facilitator (24 tests: OutputList/Freeform/Formula answers, binary response format, request format verification, error handling, edge cases)

### Storage (`storage` feature)
- **`storage_tests.rs`** — UHRP URL generation/parsing/validation/normalization, roundtrip (file→URL→hash), StorageDownloader/Uploader config, UploadableFile, cross-SDK hash/file/URL vectors

### Storage HTTP (`storage`+`http` features, wiremock)
- **`storage_http_tests.rs`** — Two-step upload flow (POST /upload → PUT presigned URL), custom retention, required headers, error handling (400/401/413/500), find_file, list_uploads, renew_file, request body verification, MIME type headers, downloader URL validation (invalid/empty/HTTP/bad checksum URLs), timeout config

### Identity (`identity` feature)
- **`identity_tests.rs`** — KnownCertificateType (9 types, cross-SDK type IDs), DisplayableIdentity, Contact CRUD, IdentityQuery builder, ContactsManager async ops (add/update/remove/search/cache), CertifierInfo, BroadcastResult, static avatar URLs, default values, config builders, camelCase JSON compatibility

### KVStore (`kvstore` feature)
- **`kvstore_integration_tests.rs`** — LocalKVStore: config/entry/token/query/options types, KVStoreInterpreter (PushDrop script extraction), signature verification, constructor/get/set/remove/has/keys/list/count/clear operations, batch ops, cross-SDK compatibility (field names, PushDrop order), edge cases (unicode, large values)
- **`kvstore_global_tests.rs`** — GlobalKVStore: construction with default/custom config, network presets, input validation (empty key/value), wallet error propagation, overlay error propagation, interpreter tests (19 tests: basic tokens, context filtering, unicode, large values, old/new format), signature verification, data model tests, cross-SDK compatibility, edge cases (special chars, JSON values, multiple tags)

### Registry (`registry` feature)
- **`registry_integration_tests.rs`** — DefinitionType (string conversion, lookup services, topics, baskets, field counts), BasketDefinitionData (builder, PushDrop encode/decode), ProtocolDefinitionData (security levels), CertificateDefinitionData (field descriptors), DefinitionData enum, TokenData, RegistryRecord, query builders, result types, RegistryClientConfig, cross-SDK PushDrop field order, constants

### Documentation (default features)
- **`readme_examples.rs`** — The README's ```rust blocks that name an `examples/<name>.rs` file are those files byte for byte, and every example file appears in the README
- **`front_page.rs`**: The README's first screen, each marked sentence worked out again from its producer in the tree, no version before the examples, the `cargo add` install line, the front files and the manifest's page fields

### Profiling (`dhat-profiling` feature)
- **`memory_profiling.rs`** — Heap allocation profiling: AES-GCM (64-16384 bytes), BRC-42/ECDH derivation, Shamir 3-of-5 and 5-of-10, ECDSA sign/verify, SHA-256/Hash160 hashing

## Running Tests

```bash
# All tests with all modules
cargo test --features full

# All tests including HTTP (requires http feature)
cargo test --features "full,http"

# By feature group
cargo test --features transaction          # Transaction, script, primitives
cargo test --features wallet               # Wallet, transaction, script, primitives
cargo test --features messages             # Messages, wallet chain
cargo test --features compat               # BIP-39/32, BSM, ECIES, Base58
cargo test --features auth                 # Auth, messages, wallet chain
cargo test --features overlay              # Overlay, wallet chain
cargo test --features storage              # Storage, overlay chain
cargo test --features registry             # Registry, overlay chain
cargo test --features kvstore              # KVStore, overlay chain
cargo test --features identity             # Identity, auth, overlay chain

# Specific test file
cargo test --test wire_method_roundtrip_tests --features wallet
cargo test --test wallet_wire_cross_sdk_tests --features wallet
cargo test --test overlay_http_tests --features "overlay,http"
cargo test --test overlay_mock_tests --features full
cargo test --test live_policy_http_tests --features "transaction,http"

# Filter by test name
cargo test --features full -- test_complete_payment_workflow

# Memory profiling (single-threaded, with output; CI runs
# RUST_TEST_THREADS=1 cargo nextest run --test memory_profiling --all-features)
cargo test --test memory_profiling --features dhat-profiling -- --test-threads=1 --nocapture
```

| **overlay** | `overlay_cross_sdk_tests`, `overlay_integration_tests`, `overlay_mock_tests`, `overlay_admin_token_ts_parity_tests`, `lookup_host_accounting_760`, `lookup_tracker_accounting_762`, `lookup_tracker_backoff_769`, `lookup_answer_normalizer_768`, `lookup_discovery_779`, `lookup_slap_admission_786`, `overlay_ship_discovery_804`, `overlay_ship_requirements_tb`, `lookup_hint_merge_796`, `lookup_freeform_precedence_815`, `lookup_limits_775`, `lookup_limits_configured_775` |

Notable multi-module test files use nested submodules:
- `transaction_tests.rs`: `beef_extended_tests`, `beef_ancestry_tests`, `cross_sdk_tests`, `merkle_path_advanced_tests`
- `wallet_tests.rs`: `key_deriver_tests`, `cached_key_deriver_tests`, `proto_wallet_tests`, `cross_sdk_tests`, `wire_protocol_tests`
- `identity_tests.rs`: `contacts_manager_tests`, `identity_client_tests`
- `wire_method_roundtrip_tests.rs`: 18 submodules covering all 28 WalletInterface methods

## Adding New Tests

- **Cross-SDK vectors**: Add JSON to `tests/vectors/`, use `#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]`, include vector index in panic messages
- **Wire protocol vectors**: Add JSON to `tests/vectors/wallet_wire/`, following `{method}-{variant}-{args|result}.json` naming convention
- **Feature gates**: Use `#![cfg(feature = "...")]` at file top, or `#![cfg(all(feature = "...", feature = "http"))]` for HTTP tests
- **HTTP tests**: Use `wiremock` crate, create broadcaster/tracker with `::with_base_url(&mock_server.uri(), network)`
- **Wire protocol**: Use loopback pattern with `WalletWireTransceiver` → `WalletWireProcessor` for method roundtrips
- **Auth peer tests**: Use channel-based loopback transport for full BRC-31 handshake testing
- **Async tests**: Use `#[tokio::test]` for anything involving ChainTracker, Broadcaster, Peer, or KVStore operations
- **Test keys**: Follow TypeScript SDK patterns: `PrivateKey(42)`, `PrivateKey(69)` for deterministic test keys
- **Protocol names**: Must be 5+ characters, use `Protocol::new(SecurityLevel::*, "name")`
- **The row**: add the file's row to `## Files` and run `python3 .github/scripts/rows_from_sources.py` for its Modules cell (#706; `tests/arch_tests_claude_rows_name_modules_706.rs` refuses a file without one); a module the binary reaches only through a caller is a judgment: add the pair to `tests/caller_modules_706.txt` when the row keeps it

## Notes on BSV vs BTC Script Vectors

Some `script_valid.json` vectors may fail execution because BSV requires push-only unlocking scripts, minimal push encoding, and has different clean stack requirements. Vectors with `flags.contains("P2SH")` are skipped. The BSV-specific `spend_valid.json` vectors should all pass.
